In this work, we present Thinker-WM, a unified multimodal world model that jointly models and generates RGB, metric depth, and robot action through a shared intermediate Unified Dynamics Transformer with modality-specific encoders and decoders. The model performs multimodal <sub>un</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> l<sub>eve</sub>l<sub>:</sub> <sub>appearance,</sub> <sub>geome</sub>t<sub>ry,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>are</sub> t<sub>rans</sub>f<sub>orme</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o</sub> <sub>a</sub> common dynamic representation and jointly evolved by shared parameters, while their distinct <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ures</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>preserve</sub>d<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>same</sub> <sub>parame</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>suppor</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>mu</sub>lti<sub>p</sub>l<sub>e</sub> f<sub>unc</sub>ti<sub>ons,</sub> i<sub>nc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> joint video–action generation, world prediction, policy inference, action-conditioned generation, <sub>v</sub>id<sub>eo-</sub>t<sub>o-ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>genera</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d i<sub>nverse</sub> d<sub>ynam</sub>i<sub>cs.</sub> T<sub>o</sub> <sub>exam</sub>i<sub>ne</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>h<sub>are</sub>d <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>cap</sub>t<sub>ures ac</sub>ti<sub>on-re</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on, we</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uce a</sub> li<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>mo</sub>ti<sub>on pro</sub>b<sub>e</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>s</sub>h<sub>ows</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>rozen</sub> <sub>v</sub>i<sub>sua</sub>l <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> <sub>recovera</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> <sub>mo</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

Thinker-WM is trained on a heterogeneous corpus comprising approximately 20K hours of egocentric video, 10K hours of real-robot data, and 1K hours of simulation data, including approximately 4K hours of real-robot data with aligned depth supervision. Ego and efector poses are <sub>enco</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as</sub> f<sub>rame-</sub>t<sub>o-</sub>f<sub>rame re</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> t<sub>rans</sub>f<sub>orms, w</sub>hil<sub>e grasp s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>tl<sub>y enco</sub>d<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e curren</sub>t <sub>man</sub>i<sub>pu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d l<sub>arge-sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>egocen</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> h<sub>uman</sub> <sub>v</sub>id<sub>eo</sub> <sub>supp</sub>li<sub>es</sub> <sub>a</sub>dditi<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>pseu</sub>d<sub>o-ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>superv</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on.</sub>

Recent work has additionally introduced explicit geometric structure into this joint generative process. GeoSem-WAM [19] au<sub>g</sub>ments joint world and action learnin<sub>g</sub> with <sub>g</sub>eometric and semantic future <sub>p</sub>rediction, <sub>s</sub>h<sub>ow</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ure</sub>d <sub>spa</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>can</sub> <sub>prov</sub>id<sub>e</sub> <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on-re</sub>l<sub>evan</sub>t <sub>superv</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> b<sub>eyon</sub>d RGB <sub>appearance.</sub> WAM4D [17] introduces li<sub>g</sub>htwei<sub>g</sub>ht s<sub>p</sub>atial re<sub>g</sub>ister tokens su<sub>p</sub>ervised b<sub>y</sub> future de<sub>p</sub>th to inject 4D <sub>g</sub>eometric <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>ors</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o a v</sub>id<sub>eo-ac</sub>ti<sub>on mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>, w</sub>hil<sub>e remov</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e geome</sub>t<sub>ry</sub> b<sub>ranc</sub>h d<sub>ur</sub>i<sub>ng ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> f<sub>or e</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>ency.</sub> Th<sub>ese resu</sub>lt<sub>s sugges</sub>t th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c geome</sub>t<sub>ry can prov</sub>id<sub>e a use</sub>f<sub>u</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erme</sub>di<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> f<sub>or connec</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> <sub>v</sub>i<sub>sua</sub>l d<sub>ynam</sub>i<sub>cs</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>p</sub>h<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erac</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> I<sub>n</sub> th<sub>ese</sub> <sub>approac</sub>h<sub>es,</sub> h<sub>owever,</sub> <sub>geome</sub>t<sub>ry</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>mar</sub>il<sub>y</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uce</sub>d as an auxiliary supervisory signal or intermediate representation rather than as a modality that is jointly <sub>genera</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d th<sub>roug</sub>h<sub>ou</sub>t th<sub>e same genera</sub>ti<sub>ve process.</sub>

DreamZero [39] <sub>p</sub>rovides the closest instance of the <sub>p</sub>retrained-backbone route. It builds a robot foundation model on top of a large image-to-video difusion backbone and jointly denoises visual trajectories and robot actions under a shared objective, showing that preserving a strong visual generation prior improves d<sub>owns</sub>t<sub>ream ro</sub>b<sub>o</sub>t <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l <sub>re</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> t<sub>o v</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on-</sub>l<sub>anguage-ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>nes.</sub> It<sub>s rema</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>i<sub>es are o</sub>f <sub>a</sub> dif<sub>eren</sub>t ki<sub>n</sub>d<sub>:</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s supp</sub>li<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y a</sub> f<sub>rozen</sub> t<sub>ex</sub>t <sub>enco</sub>d<sub>er ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an pro</sub>d<sub>uce</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e genera</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> process, the model is pretrained separately for each embodiment, and its action target stays at relative joint <sub>p</sub>ositions rather than a shared end-efector re<sub>p</sub>resentation. ## 3 Model Architecture

## 3.1 Overview: Representation-Level Unified World Model

A <sub>wor</sub>ld <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l f<sub>or em</sub>b<sub>o</sub>di<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>e</sub>lli<sub>gence mus</sub>t <sub>cap</sub>t<sub>ure more</sub> th<sub>an</sub> th<sub>e v</sub>i<sub>sua</sub>l <sub>appearance o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>u</sub>t<sub>ure.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> evolution of an embodied environment is jointly reflected in what the agent observes, the geometric structure <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e scene, an</sub>d th<sub>e ac</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>cause</sub> th<sub>e o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d <sub>c</sub>h<sub>anges.</sub> Thi<sub>n</sub>k<sub>er-</sub>WM th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s</sub> RGB <sub>v</sub>id<sub>eo,</sub> <sub>me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th<sub>, an</sub>d <sub>ro</sub>b<sub>o</sub>t <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on w</sub>ithi<sub>n a s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e genera</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> f<sub>ramewor</sub>k<sub>, ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> t<sub>rea</sub>ti<sub>ng ac</sub>ti<sub>on genera</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>as</sub> <sub>a</sub> d<sub>owns</sub>t<sub>ream</sub> <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>u</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>v</sub>i<sub>sua</sub>l <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

Gi<sub>ven an o</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> $o _ { t }$ <sub>an</sub>d <sub>a</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>con</sub>diti<sub>on</sub> $^ { c , }$ Thinker-WM models the joint distribution of future visual, geometric, and action trajectories as

$$
p \left( v _ { t + 1 : t + H } , d _ { t + 1 : t + H } , a _ { t + 1 : t + H } \vert o _ { t } , c \right) ,\tag{3}
$$

where v, $d ,$ and a denote RGB video, metric depth, and robot action, respectively. The key design principle is representation-level unification: each generative modality is first encoded by a modality-specific encoder i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o an</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erme</sub>di<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> d<sub>ynam</sub>i<sub>c represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Th<sub>ese represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> th<sub>en</sub> f<sub>ee</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o a</sub> U<sub>n</sub>ifi<sub>e</sub>d D<sub>ynam</sub>i<sub>cs</sub> Transformer (UDT) to <sub>p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ate in the shared d<sub>y</sub>namic evolution. The out<sub>p</sub>uts are subsequentl<sub>y</sub> se<sub>p</sub>arated <sub>an</sub>d d<sub>eco</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d th<sub>roug</sub>h <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>a</sub>lit<sub>y-spec</sub>ifi<sub>c genera</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> h<sub>ea</sub>d<sub>.</sub>

We re<sub>p</sub>resent all robot actions usin<sub>g</sub> a unified relative end-efector (EEF) re<sub>p</sub>resentation. For each end efector, the position and orientation at time t are expressed relative to its state at the conditioning frame:

$$

<table><tr><td>Mode</td><td>Training Detail</td><td>Role</td></tr><tr><td>WAM</td><td>joint denoising of visual streams and action joint generation</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>WM</td><td>action branch omitted</td><td>visual world model</td></tr><tr><td>VLA</td><td>first video frame kept clean</td><td>policy on the current observation</td></tr><tr><td>A2V</td><td>clean action injected, prompt dropped</td><td>action-conditioned visual generation</td></tr><tr><td>V2A</td><td>clean video injected, prompt dropped</td><td>video-conditioned action generation</td></tr><tr><td>Uncond</td><td>prompt dropped, joint denoising</td><td>unconditional branch for guidance</td></tr></table>

The function modes include joint <sub>g</sub>eneration (WAM), visual world modelin<sub>g</sub> (WM), action <sub>g</sub>eneration from the current observation (VLA), action-conditioned visual <sub>g</sub>eneration (A2V), video-conditioned action <sub>g</sub>eneration (V2A), and the unconditional branch required for classifier-free <sub>g</sub>uidance.

## 3.5 Flow-Matching Parameterization

Thi<sub>n</sub>k<sub>er-</sub>WM <sub>a</sub> li<sub>es a un</sub>ifi<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ve</sub>l<sub>oc</sub>it <sub>-</sub>fi<sub>e</sub>ld <sub>arame</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>o ever enera</sub>ti<sub>ve mo</sub>d<sub>a</sub>lit <sub>.</sub> F<sub>or mo</sub>d<sub>a</sub>lit $m \in \{ v , d , a \}$ <sub>,</sub> l<sub>e</sub>t $x _ { m }$ d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub> it<sub>s c</sub>l<sub>ean represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> $\epsilon _ { m } \sim \mathcal { N } ( 0 , I )$ <sub>, an</sub>d $\sigma _ { m } \in [ 0 , 1 ]$ <sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e no</sub>i<sub>sy s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e an</sub>d tar<sub>g</sub>et ve<sup>l</sup>oc<sup>i</sup>t<sub>y</sub> are

$$
x _ { m , \sigma _ { m } } = ( 1 - \sigma _ { m } ) x _ { m } + \sigma _ { m } \epsilon _ { m } , \qquad u _ { m } ^ { \star } = \epsilon _ { m } - x _ { m } .\tag{11}
$$

Th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>s</sub> $\hat { u } _ { m } = v _ { \theta , m } ( x _ { m , \sigma _ { m } } , \sigma _ { m } , c )$ for each generative stream, where c denotes the corresponding <sub>mu</sub>lti<sub>mo</sub>d<sub>a</sub>l <sub>con</sub>diti<sub>on</sub>i<sub>ng con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

RGB and depth follow the same flow trajectory and therefore share a common noise schedule, while the <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ream</sub> <sub>uses</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>separa</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>sc</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d<sub>u</sub>l<sub>e:</sub>

$$
\sigma ( s ) = { \frac { \mathrm { s h } \cdot s } { 1 + ( \mathrm { s h } - 1 ) \cdot s } } , \qquad s \sim \mathrm { U n i f o r m } \{ 1 , \dots , 1 0 0 0 \} ,\tag{12}
$$

with sh = 6.0 for RGB and depth and sh = 1.0 for action. The modality-specific schedules are retained at inference so that the sampling trajectories remain consistent with the corresponding training distributions.

At i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>earne</sub>d <sub>ve</sub>l<sub>oc</sub>it<sub>y</sub> fi<sub>e</sub>ld<sub>s</sub> <sub>are</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>egra</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>us</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> 50 <sub>s</sub>t<sub>eps</sub> f<sub>or</sub> RGB <sub>an</sub>d d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>an</sub>d 10 <sub>s</sub>t<sub>eps</sub> f<sub>or</sub> <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Cl<sub>ass</sub>ifi<sub>er-</sub>f<sub>ree</sub> <sub>gu</sub>id<sub>ance</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>app</sub>li<sub>e</sub>d <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>v</sub>i<sub>sua</sub>l <sub>s</sub>t<sub>reams,</sub> <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> b<sub>ranc</sub>h i<sub>s</sub> <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d without guidance to avoid instability in the generated action trajectories.

## 3.6 Training Objective

Gi<sub>ven</sub> th<sub>e ve</sub>l<sub>oc</sub>it<sub>y-</sub>fi<sub>e</sub>ld <sub>parame</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on a</sub>b<sub>ove,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> l<sub>oss</sub> f<sub>or eac</sub>h <sub>genera</sub>ti<sub>ve mo</sub>d<sub>a</sub>lit<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>d flow-matching regression objective:

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathcal { L } _ { m } ( \theta ) = \mathbb { E } _ { x _ { m } , \epsilon _ { m } , \sigma _ { m } } \left[ \| v _ { \theta , m } ( x _ { m , \sigma _ { m } } , \sigma _ { m } , c ) - u _ { m } ^ { \star } \| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } \right] . } \end{array}\tag{13}
$$

F<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e ac</sub>ti<sub>on s</sub>t<sub>ream,</sub> th<sub>e un</sub>ifi<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on space</sub> h<sub>as</sub> $U = 2 0$ di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>ons</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>a</sub> <sub>va</sub>lidit<sub>y</sub> <sub>mas</sub>k $m _ { u }$ th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>egrees o</sub>f f<sub>ree</sub>d<sub>om ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e ro</sub>b<sub>o</sub>t<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> l<sub>oss</sub> i<sub>s</sub>

$$
\mathcal { L } _ { a } ( \theta ) = \mathbb { E } \left[ \frac { \sum _ { u = 1 } ^ { U } m _ { u } \left. \hat { u } _ { a , u } - u _ { a , u } ^ { \star } \right. _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } } { \sum _ { u = 1 } ^ { U } m _ { u } + \varepsilon } \right] ,\tag{14}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $\varepsilon$ i<sub>s a sma</sub>ll <sub>numer</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bili<sub>zer.</sub> I<sub>nva</sub>lid <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>ons are ne</sub>ith<sub>er no</sub>i<sub>se</sub>d <sub>nor</sub> i<sub>nc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>oss;</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>oss</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>compu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on-</sub>t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>en</sub> <sub>space,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>va</sub>lidit<sub>y</sub> <sub>mas</sub>k i<sub>s</sub> <sub>expan</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>correspon</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>en</sub> di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>ons.</sub> Th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>a</sub>lit<sub>y-spec</sub>ifi<sub>c no</sub>i<sub>se sc</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d<sub>u</sub>l<sub>es</sub> i<sub>n</sub> S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> 3<sub>.</sub>5 <sub>are use</sub>d <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> corres<sub>p</sub>on<sup>di</sup>n<sub>g</sub> ${ \mathcal { L } } _ { m }$

At training time, the current function mode r partitions the modalities into a generated set $\mathcal { G } ( \boldsymbol { r } )$ , a <sub>con</sub>diti<sub>one</sub>d <sub>se</sub>t $\mathcal { C } ( \boldsymbol { r } )$ <sub>,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>an</sub> <sub>om</sub>itt<sub>e</sub>d <sub>se</sub>t $\mathcal { O } ( r )$ . The total objective is

$$
\mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { t o t a l } } ( \theta ) = \sum _ { m \in \mathcal { G } ( r ) } \lambda _ { m } \mathcal { L } _ { m } ( \theta ; r ) ,\tag{15}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $\lambda _ { m }$ b<sub>a</sub>l<sub>ances</sub> th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>a</sub>lit<sub>y sca</sub>l<sub>es or e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>en coun</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> F<sub>or</sub> $m \in \mathcal { C } ( r )$ , sett<sup>i</sup>n<sub>g</sub> $\sigma _ { m } = 0$ f<sub>ee</sub>d<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>a</sub>lit<sub>y as a c</sub>l<sub>ean o</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>ti<sub>on w</sub>hil<sub>e exc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> it<sub>s pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> l<sub>oss;</sub> f<sub>or</sub> $m \in { \mathcal { O } } ( r )$ <sub>,</sub> th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>a</sub>lit<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s a</sub>b<sub>sen</sub>t f<sub>rom</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>orwar</sub>d <sub>compu</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>oss.</sub> C<sub>onsequen</sub>tl<sub>y,</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>i<sub>x</sub> f<sub>unc</sub>ti<sub>on mo</sub>d<sub>es—</sub>WAM<sub>,</sub> WM<sub>,</sub> VLA<sub>,</sub> A2V<sub>,</sub> V2A<sub>, an</sub>d U<sub>ncon</sub>d<sub>—s</sub>h<sub>are</sub> th<sub>e same</sub> l<sub>oss</sub> f<sub>orm an</sub>d dif<sub>er on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> i<sub>n w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>a</sub>liti<sub>es are genera</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>,</sub> <sub>con</sub>diti<sub>one</sub>d<sub>, or om</sub>itt<sub>e</sub>d<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>es are samp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith fi<sub>xe</sub>d <sub>propor</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e g</sub>l<sub>o</sub>b<sub>a</sub>l <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ep</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ex,</sub> <sub>ensur</sub>i<sub>ng cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ency across</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a-para</sub>ll<sub>e</sub>l <sub>ran</sub>k<sub>s.</sub>

## 4 Data

Our pretraining corpus aggregates approximately 20,000 hours of high-quality egocentric human video and 10,000 hours of robot data, including around 4,000 hours with aligned depth. We organize the corpus into i<sub>n-</sub>h<sub>ouse</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a,</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erne</sub>t d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a, an</sub>d d<sub>ep</sub>th d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a.</sub> Fi<sub>gure</sub> 6 <sub>summar</sub>i<sub>zes</sub> it<sub>s compos</sub>iti<sub>on.</sub>

## 5.3 Data Preparation and Throughput Matching

R<sub>ea</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>enco</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> <sub>a</sub> b<sub>a</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h d<sub>om</sub>i<sub>na</sub>t<sub>es</sub> <sub>prepara</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>cos</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>no</sub>i<sub>se</sub> <sub>use</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>c</sub>h<sub>anges</sub> <sub>every</sub> step. We therefore encode each batch once and reuse the latents across N optimization steps, each of which d<sub>raws</sub> f<sub>res</sub>h <sub>no</sub>i<sub>se</sub> <sub>an</sub>d ti<sub>mes</sub>t<sub>ep</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>runs</sub> <sub>a</sub> f<sub>u</sub>ll f<sub>orwar</sub>d<sub>–</sub>b<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>war</sub>d <sub>pass:</sub>

$$
B _ { t } = B _ { t _ { 0 } } , \qquad t _ { 0 } = \lfloor t / N \rfloor N ,\tag{17}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $B _ { t }$ is the encoded batch at step t. The N steps are independent denoising objectives because the flow-matching target is rebuilt from fresh noise each step. This divides the encoding cost by N.

T<sub>o</sub> k<sub>eep</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> l<sub>oop</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> <sub>wa</sub>iti<sub>ng,</sub> <sub>a</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>per-ran</sub>k <sub>queue</sub> l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>a</sub> b<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>groun</sub>d <sub>pro</sub>d<sub>ucer</sub> k<sub>eep</sub> complete batches ready ahead of consumption. Because a fresh batch is requested once every N steps, <sub>prepara</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ays</sub> hidd<sub>en</sub> <sub>as</sub> l<sub>ong</sub> <sub>as</sub>

$$
C _ { \mathrm { p r e p } } \ \leq \ N \cdot C _ { \mathrm { s t e p } } ,\tag{18}
$$

## 6 Training Strategy

Thi<sub>n</sub>k<sub>er-</sub>WM <sub>suppor</sub>t<sub>s mu</sub>lti<sub>p</sub>l<sub>e con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>es an</sub>d i<sub>s</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>on</sub> h<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erogeneous</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a sources.</sub> L<sub>arge-sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>g</sub>eneral video su<sub>pp</sub>lies broad visual and tem<sub>p</sub>oral <sub>p</sub>riors [5, 8, 35]; real robot recordin<sub>g</sub>s <sub>p</sub>rovide embodiments<sub>p</sub>ecific visual statistics and the action su<sub>p</sub>ervision on which the conditionals of Eq. (3) de<sub>p</sub>end; e<sub>g</sub>ocentric human video supplies scalable task, scene, and object diversity that robot teleoperation cannot economically reach [4, 26]. The Data Factor<sub>y p</sub>rovides the execution substrate, and this section s<sub>p</sub>ecifies how these <sub>sources are com</sub>bi<sub>ne</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o a s</sub>t<sub>age</sub>d <sub>curr</sub>i<sub>cu</sub>l<sub>um.</sub>

## 6.1 Staged Training Curriculum

T<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng across a</sub>ll d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e ou</sub>t<sub>se</sub>t <sub>can</sub> d<sub>es</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bili<sub>ze op</sub>ti<sub>m</sub>i<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on, as</sub> h<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erogeneous</sub> objectives compete for the capacity of the shared model and may pull the representation toward dominant t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub> F<sub>u</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>sequen</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>l t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng,</sub> i<sub>n</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ras</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>r</sub>i<sub>s</sub>k<sub>s</sub> <sub>ca</sub>t<sub>as</sub>t<sub>rop</sub>hi<sub>c</sub> f<sub>orge</sub>tti<sub>ng</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>capa</sub>biliti<sub>es</sub> ac<sub>q</sub>uired in earlier sta<sub>g</sub>es [15, 20]. To balance ac<sub>q</sub>uisition and retention, Thinker-WM ado<sub>p</sub>ts a sta<sub>g</sub>ed <sub>curr</sub>i<sub>cu</sub>l<sub>um w</sub>ith li<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>re</sub>h<sub>earsa</sub>l<sub>, progress</sub>i<sub>ve</sub>l<sub>y</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>i<sub>ng new</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>ons an</sub>d <sub>capa</sub>biliti<sub>es</sub> while maintainin<sub>g</sub> a small re<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub> stream from earlier sta<sub>g</sub>es [2, 28]. ## 7 Motion Probe

Chapters 3 and 6 establish that Thinker-WM jointly models RGB, depth, and action through a shared Unified D<sub>y</sub>namics Transformer (UDT). A natural question is whether the shared re<sub>p</sub>resentation trul<sub>y</sub> captures cross-modal coupling, or merely processes modalities in parallel. We introduce Motion Probe, a li<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht di<sub>agnos</sub>ti<sub>c mo</sub>d<sub>u</sub>l<sub>e</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>es w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er mo</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on can</sub> b<sub>e recovere</sub>d f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>h<sub>are</sub>d <sub>v</sub>id<sub>eo represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> th<sub>ere</sub>b<sub>y prov</sub>idi<sub>ng</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>t <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence</sub> f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>veness o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> UDT<sub>.</sub> M<sub>o</sub>ti<sub>on pro</sub>b<sub>e</sub>’<sub>s</sub> <sub>e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t i<sub>s eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> 8<sub>.</sub>2<sub>.</sub>4<sub>.</sub>

The aligned teacher is frozen and cached. (b) Probing the frozen UDT: a lightweight Motion Head predicts end-efector di<sub>sp</sub>l<sub>acemen</sub>t f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>h<sub>are</sub>d <sub>v</sub>id<sub>eo represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> UDT <sub>we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>s rema</sub>i<sub>n</sub> f<sub>rozen, an</sub>d <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> M<sub>o</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> H<sub>ea</sub>d i<sub>s</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d<sub>.</sub> L<sub>ow pro</sub>b<sub>e error</sub> i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e s</sub>h<sub>are</sub>d <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on enco</sub>d<sub>es ac</sub>ti<sub>on-re</sub>l<sub>evan</sub>t <sub>mo</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

$$
\tilde { C } _ { t } = s R C _ { t } + t .\tag{21}
$$

Si<sub>nce</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>pro</sub>b<sub>e</sub> i<sub>s</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>on</sub> t<sub>empora</sub>l di<sub>sp</sub>l<sub>acemen</sub>t<sub>,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>rans</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>cance</sub>l<sub>s</sub> <sub>a</sub>ft<sub>er</sub> dif<sub>erenc</sub>i<sub>ng:</sub>

$$

W<sub>e</sub> <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th f<sub>rom</sub> t<sub>wo</sub> <sub>comp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>ary</sub> <sub>perspec</sub>ti<sub>ves:</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>accuracy</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>genera</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>geome</sub>t<sub>ry</sub> <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>ene</sub>fit <sub>o</sub>f RGB<sub>–</sub>d<sub>ep</sub>th i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> f<sub>or</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ose</sub>d<sub>-</sub>l<sub>oop</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>.</sub>

Depth generation accuracy. We compare Thinker-WM with RynnWorld-4D [41] on 48 AgiBot validation <sub>sequences.</sub> R<sub>ynn</sub>W<sub>or</sub>ld<sub>-</sub>4D <sub>a</sub>l<sub>so</sub> i<sub>nc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>d<sub>es</sub> A<sub>g</sub>iB<sub>o</sub>t i<sub>n</sub> it<sub>s</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>corpus.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>genera</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>us</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> absolute relative error (AbsRel) and root mean squared error (RMSE). As shown in Table 8, Thinker-WM achieves an AbsRel of 0.1360 and an RMSE of 0.1790 m, compared with 0.1625 and 0.2075 m for RynnWorld-4D. These correspond to relative error reductions of 16.3% and 13.7%, respectively.

As a valid-range sensitivity check, restricting RynnWorld-4D’s evaluation range from 0.05–5.0 m to the 0.15–4.0 m range used by Thinker-WM changes its AbsRel only from 0.162517 to 0.162474. Thus, the <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d Ab<sub>s</sub>R<sub>e</sub>l i<sub>mprovemen</sub>t i<sub>s</sub> <sub>no</sub>t <sub>exp</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> thi<sub>s</sub> dif<sub>erence</sub> i<sub>n</sub> <sub>va</sub>lid d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>range.</sub>

Bidirectional interaction achieves 75.0%, improving over the RGB-only and one-way variants by 15.0 and 11.7 percentage points, respectively. We further verify the gradient measurements at the objective level. Under identical batches and noise realizations, the video loss of the joint WAM mode is 0.230, compared with 0.232 for the video-only WM mode, indicating that jointly modeling action does not introduce a measurable degradation in the video objective. Conversely, providing a ground-truth action sequence as a clean condition reduces the video loss by 0.016 relative to the video-only mode, while the inverse-dynamics mode reduces the action loss by 0.010 relative to the policy mode. These changes follow the intended role of the conditional modes and provide an objective-level counterpart to the measured gradient geometry.

## 8.3.2 Trajectory Transfer and Action Recovery

S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> 8<sub>.</sub>2<sub>.</sub>1 <sub>s</sub>h<sub>ows</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>consumes an ac</sub>ti<sub>on sequence w</sub>h<sub>en one</sub> i<sub>s ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>wo cases</sub> below illustrate this behavior on recorded trajectories rather than synthetic actions.

In the first case, generation is conditioned on a real recorded trajectory. The source is a bimanual <sub>ep</sub>i<sub>so</sub>d<sub>e</sub> d<sub>om</sub>i<sub>na</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> l<sub>e</sub>ft<sub>-arm mo</sub>ti<sub>on, w</sub>ith th<sub>e w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow c</sub>h<sub>osen</sub> f<sub>or max</sub>i<sub>ma</sub>l <sub>en</sub>d<sub>-e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>or pa</sub>th l<sub>eng</sub>th<sub>.</sub> The model receives the first frame of an evaluation sample together with that recorded trajectory, so the scene and the trajectory come from diferent recordings. This tests whether the model follows the supplied trajectory rather than reproducing the motion originally performed in that scene.

## 9 Conclusion and Future Work

This work resented Thinker-WM, a unified multimodal world model that jointl models RGB, de th, <sub>an</sub>d <sub>ro</sub>b<sub>o</sub>t <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on w</sub>ithi<sub>n a s</sub>h<sub>are</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erme</sub>di<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> M<sub>o</sub>d<sub>a</sub>lit<sub>y-spec</sub>ifi<sub>c enco</sub>d<sub>ers an</sub>d d<sub>eco</sub>d<sub>ers</sub> <sub>p</sub>reserve the distinct structure of each modalit<sub>y</sub>, while a shared Unified D<sub>y</sub>namics Transformer (UDT) models their joint evolution with a single set of weights. This design supports multiple world-action functions under one parameterization, including joint video-action generation, world modeling, policy i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence, ac</sub>ti<sub>on-con</sub>diti<sub>one</sub>d <sub>genera</sub>ti<sub>on, v</sub>id<sub>eo-</sub>t<sub>o-ac</sub>ti<sub>on genera</sub>ti<sub>on, an</sub>d i<sub>nverse</sub> d<sub>ynam</sub>i<sub>cs.</sub> T<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>on</sub> <sub>approx</sub>i<sub>ma</sub>t<sub>e</sub>l<sub>y</sub> 20K h<sub>ours</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>egocen</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> <sub>v</sub>id<sub>eo,</sub> 10K h<sub>ours</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>ro</sub>b<sub>o</sub>t d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d 1K h<sub>ours</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>s</sub>i<sub>mu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a,</sub> <sub>a</sub>b<sub>ou</sub>t 4K h<sub>ours w</sub>ith <sub>a</sub>li<sub>gne</sub>d d<sub>ep</sub>th<sub>,</sub> Thi<sub>n</sub>k<sub>er-</sub>WM <sub>uses a s</sub>t<sub>age</sub>d <sub>curr</sub>i<sub>cu</sub>l<sub>um w</sub>ith <sub>rep</sub>l<sub>ay</sub> t<sub>o s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bili<sub>ze s</sub>h<sub>are</sub>d <sub>re resen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons an</sub>d <sub>s ec</sub>i<sub>a</sub>li<sub>ze</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>e</sub> l<sub>o e</sub>d <sub>ro</sub>b<sub>o</sub>t<sub>.</sub> A<sub>cross</sub> b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k<sub>s,</sub> it <sub>ac</sub>hi<sub>eves</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>es</sub>t O<sub>vera</sub>ll <sub>score</sub> on EWMBench (4.66), ranks first on WorldOlympiad Robotics with an All score of 0.740, and obtains the highest 12-metric EWMScore of 71.73 on WorldArena Track-1. Supplying a recorded action sequence as a clean condition improves Overall from 4.66 to 5.03, with HSD increasing from 0.5638 to 0.7369 and nDTW from 0.7102 to 0.8736.
